47 claims about what LabPlanner does

Generated by bin/c6-report --write on 2026-09-17. Every table and message below came out of a real run made while building this page — nothing is transcribed.

Each numbered sentence is a claim about what the toolkit does at a bench. The evidence for it is underneath. Answer true if that is what should happen, false if a person who knows the chemistry would do it differently.

False is the useful answer. 792 tests pass; every one of them asserts that a number did not change, and not one knows whether pS-A is the right thing to write on a tube. That is the only question this page asks.

The button at the bottom puts your answers in a box to copy. Or just say the numbers.

What goes on a tube

1

When four colonies are picked into tubes, each tube is labelled with the construct name and a clone letter — pS-A, pS-B, pS-C, pS-D — and not a short code.

answered 2026-09-15

The alternative, used until 2026-09-15, was the next letter of a per-experiment sequence (L3h). Two naming schemes on one object.

2

That same name follows the material: the colony and the miniprep made from it both say pS-A, and the sequencing tube adds only the read direction — pS-AF.

answered 2026-09-15

A label that changes at each step has to be cross-referenced at each step. The sequencing tube leaves the building and comes back as a file named for what was written on it.

9

Thirty-two clones asked into one 24-well block is refused rather than seated somehow, and the message names the count and the vessel.

answered 2026-09-15

Which sitting a second block belongs to is a scheduling decision, not a layout one.

$ c6-packet
c6-packet: session 6 (Picking) puts more than 24 clones in one 24-well block. Two blocks is a decision about the session, not about the layout — split the picking across two sittings, or say a larger `vessel=` on the line.

The gel before an assembly makes no new tube and nothing is cut out of it. A few µL come out of each PCR tube for the gel, and the PCR tube itself carries on to the cleanup — so the gel row shows which tube it LOADS, not a tube it produces.

needs an answer

A gel can be either: a check, or a purification you cut a band out of. They need different plasticware, different volumes, and the second consumes the sample. The sheet states which one this is rather than leaving it to the reader.

Where four constructs are each picked four times, the picking sheet lists all SIXTEEN clones in one 24-well block, wells A1 through D4, and the note above the table says sixteen — the same number as the rows under it.

needs an answer

It said FOUR until 2026-09-17. The note took its count from one construct’s clone number while the table held four constructs binned onto one sheet, so the sentence describing the layout disagreed with the layout. 17

A kanamycin transformation is given three control plates beside the real one, and the sheet says what each one would prove.

answered 2026-09-15

A blank plate has four causes; the controls are how you tell them apart.

Controls — three plates, the same for every kan transformation

platewhat goes on itit answers
kan +Mach1 + the kan control plasmidthe cells are competent and took up DNA
kan −Mach1, no DNA addedthe plate is not simply growing untransformed cells
kan streakthe kan control strain, streaked — not transformedanything could have grown on this batch of kan plates

The sequence analysis sheet arrives with its result and explanation columns EMPTY — one row per clone, for a person to fill in — and a fixed list of result tokens above it to choose from: Perfect, Perfect Partial, Silent Mutation, Missense Mutation and the rest.

needs an answer

The verdict is the one thing in the packet the compiler cannot compute. A fixed vocabulary is what makes the answer readable by the next session rather than a sentence somebody wrote in their own words.

Result tokens — use one of these exactly

tokenwhat it means
PerfectFull region covered, matches the model.
Perfect PartialMatches where readable, but the region is not fully covered.
Silent MutationBase change in the ORF, same amino acid.
Missense MutationBase change in the ORF, the amino acid changes.
Nonsense MutationBase change in the ORF, creates a stop codon.
IndelInsertion or deletion in the region — may frameshift.
Mixed CloneTwo sequences in one trace; the colony was not clonal.
FailedRead unusable, or it will not align to the target.

Below the per-clone table the analysis sheet has a second, one-row table: the single clone you are most confident about, and why. Every session after this one fetches that tube.

needs an answer

Four verdicts do not say which tube to use next. The sheet asks for the choice in writing, and says on the page why: "if the answer is not on this sheet it is in somebody’s memory".

The picking sheet tells you to photograph the plates under blue and ambient light BEFORE picking, and says why: the photographs are the record of what you chose between.

needs an answer

Once the colonies are picked the plate is spent and what else was on it is gone. If a clone turns out wrong later, the only way to ask whether a better colony was there is the photograph.

Picking and inoculation for Experiment four-constructs — on the page

  • Photograph the plates under blue and ambient before picking. The photographs are the record of what you chose between.

What the electroporation sheet does carry is one row per construct with the host, the antibiotic and the temperature the CHARACTERIZATION file asked for — B.subtilis, Kan, 30 °C — not the ones the cloning transformation used.

needs an answer

The cloning host and the host being tested are different organisms with different selection and different growth temperatures. Carrying the cloning step’s values forward would plate B.subtilis at 37 on the E. coli marker.

An oligo held at 50 µM — neither the working strength nor the stock — is not diluted silently. The sheet says what was actually found and leaves the arithmetic to a person.

answered 2026-09-17

A 10 µM working stock is made from a 100 µM one by a known dilution. From 50 µM it is a different sum, and guessing which the tube really is puts the wrong primer concentration in every reaction on the sheet.

Where the same plasmid sits in the freezer minipreped at all four stages — primary, secondary, tertiary and quaternary — the sheet sends somebody to the TERTIARY. Not the freshest tube, and not the cleanest-sounding one.

answered 2026-09-17

Your ruling on 2026-09-17: "The secondary is much cleaner than the primary. The tertiary is about as good as the secondary, and carries less risk of running out of the precious clean secondary. A quaternary or higher is starting to potentially risk drift. So, I would make the order 3>2>1>4+." The code used to score every stage as one better than the last, so a quaternary outranked everything — nothing had ever asked whether later kept being better past the tertiary.

Your "nice simple plan" on 2026-09-17, built. Across seventy-two names of the form <organism><number>, the share of pairs that collide goes from 1.4% to 0.4%. But TWO CHARACTERS IS A CEILING and this does not remove the problem: twenty-one usable second characters means a lab running twenty experiments at once still has about a one-in-two chance that some pair shares a prefix. What it refuses, and what it says

37

The electroporation sheet says outright that no electroporation protocol is in the library, so it carries the conditions and NOT the procedure — naming what it does not tell you: the cuvette gap, the voltage, the recovery medium.

needs an answer

The alternative is a sheet that looks complete and is not. Somebody would take it to a bench and find out at the cuvette. Naming the three missing numbers is what makes the gap actionable rather than a vague disclaimer.

A construct name too long for a tube cap is a WARNING and the experiment still compiles, because the name came from the file and that is the author’s to choose.

answered 2026-09-15
$ c6-check
note   Construction of pVeryLongConstructName.txt
          LONG_NAME [step 2]
          the file's product is "pVeryLongConstructName", which is 22 characters. That name goes on a 1.5 mL cap with a clone designation after it — "pVeryLongConstructName-A" — and on the sequencing tube after that. 6 or fewer is the aim and 8 is about the limit of what somebody will write by hand. Longer is writeable and less legible, and this is the moment to think about it: after the file is written the name is the key every later step resolves through.

The order of the work

44

The sessions come out in an order somebody could actually work: stocks and dilutions first, then PCR, then the gel and assembly, then the transformation, and the sequence analysis before anything that uses a verified plasmid.

Mistakes that used to pass silently

46

A construction file that says `GoldenGate frag1 …` on line 1 and `PCR … frag1` on line 2 — consuming a fragment the NEXT line makes — is now refused, naming both lines.

answered 2026-09-15

JCA, 2026-09-16: "Are you imagining a scenario where they first say: GoldenGate frag1 and then next line say PCR frag1? That would be an invalid construction file." It is, and nothing said so: the check existed and was structurally dead, because the map of what is produced was filled AS the walk went, so when line 1 was examined line 2’s product was not in it yet and the test could never be true.

inventory.txt
this is not a freezer, it is a sentence
nor is this

How much of this did anybody look at

Scenarios are unbounded; decisions are not. Nobody can list the experiments a lab will write. But every one of them is compiled by a countable set of decisions, and that set can be listed and watched. So "have we covered every scenario?" has no answer, and "which decisions has nobody looked at?" does.

Two different things, and the second is what this page is for: a run took this path a claim above asserts something about it, and you answered. The suite gives the first. Only you give the second.

in the toolkita run reachesa claim asks about
rules in the rule tables5654
not measurable — see below
design modules1414
14
problem codes1715
10

47 claims drawn from 28 of the runs in the matrix.

The gaps, by name

No designs no claim asks about
— every one of them runs in the matrix and is asserted by unit tests. Nobody has been shown what they put on a page. This is the largest gap and it is the one worth closing next.
Five reported problems no claim asks about
AMBIGUOUS_ACROSS_FILES, ANALYSIS_VERIFIES_WHAT, DANGLING_PRODUCT, DUPLICATE_INPUT, LEGACY_FORMAT — each fires, and nobody has ruled on whether the sentence it prints is the right sentence.
Two problems nothing reaches
PARSE_FAILED, SESSION_OUTGROWS_VESSEL — no example produces either. SESSION_OUTGROWS_VESSEL became unreachable when allocateWells landed, which is the good outcome; it is named here rather than deleted, because the day it fires again is the day a compile started colliding.
Two rules nothing reaches
label.noSide, labelUniqueness.twoOfAKindOneSitting — held to exactly this set by scenarios.test.js, so a third one appearing fails the suite.

Why "a claim asks about it" is not measurable for rules. One scenario fires about forty rules. A claim pinning one sentence of that run has asserted nothing about the other thirty-nine. An earlier draft of this table counted them all and reported 52 of 54, which looked excellent and only meant the runs behind the claims are ordinary experiments.

And what none of it can see. Two kinds of defect, both of which have already happened here. Correct decisions composing badly — four constructs each picked into well A1, every rule right, the combination wrong, full coverage on both rules throughout. And a decision that should exist and does not: your ruling that "not all these controls are needed when transforming miniprep DNA" was a missing distinction, and no count of what exists will ever find what is absent. That is what reading the claims does and the numbers cannot.
